(MEDITECH) — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-17\n     4→Developer website: https://www.meditech.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Medical Information Technology, Inc. (MEDITECH) is one of the largest and longest-established EHR vendors in the United States. Founded in 1969 by A. Neil Pappalardo and colleagues, the company is privately held and headquartered in Westwood, Massachusetts. MEDITECH reported approximately $494 million in revenue (2019) and employs around 3,000–3,500 people. It is the third-largest U.S. acute care hospital EHR vendor with approximately 14.8% market share as of 2024 (behind Epic at ~42% and Oracle Health at ~23%), serving an estimated 630–690 U.S. hospitals and over 1,000 organizations worldwide. MEDITECH has won Best in KLAS for 11+ consecutive years, especially dominant in the small hospital (1–150 bed) category.\n     9→\n    10→MEDITECH has gone through several major technology platform generations over its history: MAGIC (1979, MUMPS-based green-screen), Client/Server (1990s, thick-client architecture), 6.x (2000s-2010s), and Expanse (current generation, web-based). All four platform generations have active ONC certifications, though MAGIC and Client/Server are legacy systems receiving only minimal compliance updates. The company's most prominent customer is HCA Healthcare, which operates 190+ hospitals and has historically run MEDITECH MAGIC but is now migrating to Expanse. MEDITECH also serves community hospitals, critical access hospitals, academic medical centers, long-term care facilities, and international health systems (e.g., UPMC Ireland, Fraser Health Authority in British Columbia, Aga Khan University Hospital in Kenya).\n    11→\n    12→MEDITECH is notable for being a comprehensive, fully integrated hospital information system — not just an EHR. Its product list spans clinical, diagnostic, administrative, financial, and operational domains, all within a single integrated platform. This is important context for EHI export evaluation: the certified modules (Core HCIS, Ambulatory, ED Management) are components of a much larger integrated system that also includes billing, lab, pharmacy, imaging, dietary, HR/payroll, materials management, and more.\n    13→\n    14→---\n    15→\n    16→## Product: MEDITECH Expanse\n    17→\n    18→CHPL IDs: 10925 (Expanse 2.2 Ambulatory), 10926 (Expanse 2.2 Core HCIS), 10927 (Expanse 2.2 ED Management), 10929 (Expanse 2.1 Core HCIS), 10930 (Expanse ED Management v2.1), 11742 (Expanse 2.1 Ambulatory)\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→MEDITECH Expanse is the company's current-generation, web-based EHR platform. It is a comprehensive, fully integrated hospital and health system information platform — not merely a clinical EHR, but an enterprise-wide system covering clinical care, revenue cycle, patient engagement, analytics, and business operations. The certified modules (Core HCIS, Ambulatory, ED Management) are components of this much larger platform.\n    23→\n    24→Expanse is available as a traditional licensed implementation (on-premises or private cloud) or as MEDITECH as a Service (MaaS), a cloud-hosted subscription model. MaaS saw 21% growth in 2024 and is particularly popular with smaller and rural hospitals. Expanse supports FHIR R4 APIs and interoperability through its Traverse Exchange network and Greenfield Workspace developer environment.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→Expanse serves health systems of all sizes, from small critical access hospitals to large integrated delivery networks. Key deployments include:\n    29→- **HCA Healthcare** — 190+ hospitals, 2,300+ sites of care across 20 states and the UK (large-scale Expanse migration from MAGIC)\n    30→- **Emanate Health** — largest nonprofit in California's San Gabriel Valley\n    31→- **Frederick Health** — independent community healthcare organization with precision medicine program\n    32→- **Hebrew SeniorLife** — largest nonprofit senior care provider in New England\n    33→- **Fraser Health Authority** (British Columbia) — one of the first to deploy generative AI clinical documentation in Expanse\n    34→- **UPMC Ireland** — 4 hospitals, 2 cancer centres, 6 sports medicine clinics\n    35→- **Aga Khan University Hospital, Nairobi** — academic medical center going fully paperless across 7 hospitals and 340+ outreach centres\n    36→- **Centre for Neuro Skills** — specialty brain injury rehabilitation (7 locations, via MaaS)\n    37→- **Bethany Children's Health Center** — 160-bed pediatric rehabilitation hospital (via MaaS)\n    38→\n    39→Day-to-day users include physicians, nurses, pharmacists, therapists, social workers, lab technicians, radiologists, billing staff, coders, registration clerks, case managers, practice managers, and administrative/executive staff. Patients interact via the MyHealth patient portal, MHealth mobile app, and Health Records on iPhone.\n    40→\n    41→Expanse is KLAS #1 for Acute Care EHR (Small 1–150 Bed) for 6 consecutive years and ranks in the top 2 for Overall Health System Suite. It represents two sub-versions: 6.0 (v6.08c) and 6.1 (v6.15c). Like Expanse, it is a comprehensive integrated hospital information system, not just a clinical EHR module. The 6.x platform was the bridge between MEDITECH's older Client/Server architecture and the modern web-based Expanse. It introduced improved graphical interfaces and some web capabilities but is not fully web-based like Expanse.\n   155→\n   156→The certified modules (Core HCIS, ED Management, Ambulatory) are components of the full 6.x platform, which includes the same broad range of clinical, financial, and administrative modules described under Expanse (though with older user interfaces and fewer modern features like AI, mobile apps, and cloud hosting).\n   157→\n   158→### Users & Market\n   159→\n   160→MEDITECH 6.x is still in active use at many hospitals that have not yet migrated to Expanse. According to KLAS data, 63% of legacy MEDITECH customers making a new EHR decision in 2024 chose to migrate to Expanse, meaning a significant portion remain on 6.x or are in the process of transitioning. The user base includes community hospitals, regional health systems, and some larger organizations that adopted 6.x before Expanse was available. The same range of clinical and administrative staff use 6.x as use Expanse.\n   161→\n   162→### Modules & Functionality\n   163→\n   164→The 6.x platform includes functionally similar modules to Expanse — clinical EHR, lab, pharmacy, imaging, revenue cycle, practice management, business operations, etc. — but without Expanse's modern features (web-based UI, AI/ambient listening, mobile-first apps, cloud-native MaaS, advanced interoperability via Traverse Exchange). The certification page shows that 6.x deployments also use the same shared modules: Continuity of Care Interface (CCD), MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interfaces for immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, electronic case reporting, and cancer case reporting.\n   165→\n   166→The 6.1 platform also has a separately certified Oncology module (MEDITECH 6.1 Oncology v6.15c), indicating integrated cancer care management.\n   167→\n   168→### Data & Content\n   169→\n   170→The data stored by MEDITECH 6.x is essentially the same categories as Expanse (clinical records, lab results, pharmacy data, billing/claims, scheduling, patient portal messages, care coordination documents, business operations data, etc.), since it runs the same integrated suite of modules. The primary differences from Expanse are in the user interface, technology architecture, and modern feature set — not in the fundamental data domains.\n   171→\n   172→---\n   173→\n   174→## Product: MEDITECH MAGIC\n   175→\n   176→CHPL IDs: 10979 (MAGIC Core HCIS), 10981 (MAGIC ED Management), 11018 (MAGIC HCA Core HCIS without PatientKeeper)\n   177→\n   178→### What It Is\n   179→\n   180→MEDITECH MAGIC is the company's legacy platform, originally launched in 1979. It is built on the MUMPS programming language (also known as M/MIIS) and features a character-based \"green-screen\" user interface. Despite its age, MAGIC remains in active use at some hospitals, particularly those that have not yet migrated to newer MEDITECH platforms. MAGIC is an on-premises system and does not support web-based or mobile workflows.\n   181→\n   182→The \"MAGIC HCA\" variant (CHPL 11018) is a special certification for HCA Healthcare's deployment of MAGIC without the PatientKeeper mobile overlay. This variant has notably different certified criteria from the standard MAGIC product, including (b)(1) Transitions of Care — Send, (b)(2) Transitions of Care — Receive, (e)(1) View/Download/Transmit (patient portal), and (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5) for public health reporting (immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, cancer case reporting). This suggests HCA's MAGIC deployment includes additional interoperability and patient-facing capabilities not present in the standard MAGIC certification.\n   183→\n   184→PatientKeeper is a third-party clinician-facing mobile application that integrates with MEDITECH systems (both MAGIC and Expanse) to provide physicians with smartphone/tablet access to patient data, lab results, notes, and workflow tools. HCA has historically used PatientKeeper as a mobile overlay on top of MAGIC's text-based interface.\n   185→\n   186→### Users & Market\n   187→\n   188→MAGIC's primary remaining user base consists of hospitals that have not yet migrated to newer MEDITECH platforms. The most notable MAGIC user is HCA Healthcare, which operated 190+ hospitals on MAGIC and is actively migrating to Expanse. As of 2024, HCA had completed Expanse rollout to 43+ hospitals. MAGIC is only receiving minimal updates for compliance and patient safety; it is no longer the focus of MEDITECH's development. The system is on a sunset trajectory.\n   189→\n   190→### Modules & Functionality\n   191→\n   192→MAGIC, like all MEDITECH platforms, is a comprehensive integrated hospital information system. The certification page lists MAGIC-specific certifications for: Core HCIS, ED Management, Medical and Practice Management (MPM) — an ambulatory/practice management module, Oncology, and the shared CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules. MAGIC supports the full range of hospital operations — clinical documentation, lab, pharmacy, imaging, billing, scheduling, etc. — but through its legacy green-screen interface.\n   193→\n   194→The MAGIC platform also includes the same Business Operations modules (GL, AP, HR, Payroll, Materials Management, etc.) as the broader MEDITECH suite, as these are part of the integrated HCIS platform.\n   195→\n   196→### Data & Content\n   197→\n   198→MAGIC stores the same fundamental categories of healthcare data as the newer platforms: clinical records, lab results, pharmacy data, orders, billing/claims, scheduling, patient registration, supply chain, HR/payroll, etc. The data model is rooted in MUMPS/M databases rather than modern relational or cloud databases, but the functional data domains are comparable. The HCA variant's additional certifications for (e)(1) patient portal access and (b)(1)/(b)(2) transitions of care confirm that patient portal data and clinical document exchange data are also stored.\n   199→\n   200→---\n   201→\n   202→## Product: MEDITECH Client/Server\n   203→\n   204→CHPL IDs: 10982 (C/S Core HCIS), 10984 (C/S ED Management)\n   205→\n   206→### What It Is\n   207→\n   208→MEDITECH Client/Server (C/S) is the platform generation that succeeded MAGIC in the 1990s. It introduced a graphical thick-client architecture replacing MAGIC's green-screen interface, but it is still a legacy platform that predates the web-based 6.x and Expanse systems. Like MAGIC, Client/Server is on-premises only and is receiving only minimal compliance updates. It shares the v5.67c software version with MAGIC, suggesting convergence in the codebase at this point.\n   209→\n   210→The certification page lists Client/Server certifications for: Core HCIS, ED Management, Medical and Practice Management (MPM), Oncology, and the shared CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules.\n   211→\n   212→### Users & Market\n   213→\n   214→Client/Server's user base consists of hospitals that adopted MEDITECH after MAGIC but before 6.x. Like MAGIC users, many are migrating to Expanse. The system is on a sunset trajectory with minimal development investment.\n   215→\n   216→### Modules & Functionality\n   217→\n   218→Client/Server includes the same comprehensive suite of integrated hospital modules as other MEDITECH platforms — clinical EHR, lab, pharmacy, imaging, revenue cycle, practice management, business operations, etc. — through a graphical thick-client interface. The certification page confirms C/S also has separately certified Oncology, MPM, CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules.\n   219→\n   220→### Data & Content\n   221→\n   222→Data domains are comparable to the other MEDITECH platforms: clinical records, diagnostic results, pharmacy data, billing/revenue cycle, scheduling, administrative/business data. The data architecture is legacy but the functional scope is the same integrated HCIS.\n   223→\n   224→---\n   225→\n   226→## Cross-Platform Observations\n   227→\n   228→### Shared Components\n   229→All four platforms share certain separately certified modules that are cross-compatible:\n   230→- **MEDITECH Continuity of Care Interface (CCD)** — handles clinical document exchange (C-CDAs)\n   231→- **MEDITECH MyHealth Portal** (v2.0c) — patient portal for all platforms\n   232→- **Public Health Interfaces** — immunization registry transmission, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, electronic case reporting, cancer case reporting\n   233→\n   234→### Certification Criteria Analysis\n   235→All products have nearly identical certified criteria, including:\n   236→- **(a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)**: Core clinical capabilities (CPOE, demographics, problem list, medication list, medication allergy list, family health history, implantable device list)\n   237→- **(b)(3), (b)(10), (b)(11)**: Electronic prescribing, EHI export, care plan interoperability\n   238→- **(c)(1)–(c)(3)**: Clinical quality measures\n   239→- **(d) series**: Privacy, security, auditing\n   240→- **(e)(3)**: Patient health information capture\n   241→- **(g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)**: API access, FHIR\n   242→\n   243→The MAGIC HCA variant (11018) uniquely includes (b)(1), (b)(2) transitions of care, (e)(1) patient portal access, and (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5) public health reporting — suggesting HCA's deployment has enhanced interoperability capabilities.\n   244→\n   245→### Key Takeaway for EHI Export Assessment\n   246→The certified modules (Core HCIS, Ambulatory, ED Management) are just three components of an enormous integrated hospital information system. The full MEDITECH platform includes dozens of modules spanning clinical care, diagnostics, pharmacy, revenue cycle, patient engagement, care coordination, specialty care (oncology, L&D, mental health, home health, hospice, surgical services, critical care, dietary), and business operations (GL, AP, HR, payroll, materials management, staffing). Under §170.315(b)(10), the EHI export should cover all electronic health information stored by the product \"of which the Health IT Module is a part\" — meaning the entire integrated MEDITECH system, not just the certified module.\n   247→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. ehr.meditech.com/meditech-product-list) describes an extensive set of integrated modules:\n\n**Clinical Solutions:**\n- Ambulatory (outpatient EHR)\n- Electronic Health Record (inpatient)\n- Emergency interventions)\n- Expanse Navigator (AI-powered search and summarization of the patient record, built on Google Cloud)\n- Genomics/Precision Medicine\n- Order Management (CPOE, medication reconciliation)\n- Outpatient Services\n- Pharmacy\n- Physician Care Manager\n- Therapies\n\n**Diagnostic Services:**\n- Blood Bank\n- Imaging and Documentation Management\n- Laboratory\n- Microbiology\n- Outreach Lab\n- Pathology\n- Phlebotomy\n\n**Care Coordination and Patient Engagement:**\n- Care Compass (population health patient registries)\n- Case Management\n- Community Care Transitions Portal\n- Expanse Patient Connect (cloud-based two-way patient texting)\n- MyHealth (patient portal)\n- Telehealth\n- Traverse Exchange (interoperability network for cross-organization data exchange)\n- Virtual Care (including Virtual On Demand Care)\n\n**Specialty Care:**\n- Critical Care\n- Dietary\n- Home Health\n- Hospice\n- Labor and Delivery\n- Long Term Care (Continuing Care)\n- Mental Health\n- Oncology\n- Surgical Services\n\n**Patient Access and Revenue Cycle:**\n- Abstracting\n- Health Information Management (HIM)\n- Practice Management\n- Quality and Risk Management\n- Registration\n- Revenue Cycle / Electronic Claims\n- Scanning and Archiving\n- Scheduling and Referral Management\n- Expanse Transport\n\n**Business Operations:**\n- Accounts Payable\n- Budgeting and Forecasting\n- Business and Clinical Analytics (BCA)\n- Corporate Management Software\n- Data Repository\n- Executive Support System\n- Fixed Assets\n- General Ledger\n- Human Resources\n- Materials Management\n- Payroll/Personnel\n- Staff Gateway\n- Staffing and Scheduling\n\nMEDITECH also delivers standard evidence-based content across 50+ medical specialties, including order sets, documentation templates, flowsheets, widgets, protocols, and surveillance profiles (e.g., Cardiology, Pediatrics, Neurology).\n\nAdditional capabilities described in MEDITECH marketing and the Tegria guide: ambient listening for clinical documentation, auto-generation of discharge summaries (hospital course narrative), predictive analytics (Surveillance module identifying high-risk patients), Apple Health Records on iPhone integration, Google Workspace for Healthcare integration, and Expanse Virtual Assistant for touchless voice search.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the modules and features described above, MEDITECH Expanse stores an exceptionally broad range of healthcare data:\n\n**Clinical data** (per EHR, Ambulatory, ED, specialty modules): Patient demographics, encounter/visit records, problem lists, diagnoses, allergies, medications, medication administration records, vital signs, clinical notes (physician, nursing, therapy, social work), orders (lab, radiology, medications, procedures), clinical decision support alerts, care plans, flowsheets, assessments, immunizations, growth charts, surgical/procedure records, anesthesia records, labor & delivery records, critical care flowsheets, oncology treatment plans/infusion records, mental health assessments, home health/hospice documentation, dietary orders and assessments.\n\n**Diagnostic data** (per Lab, Microbiology, Pathology, Blood Bank, Imaging, Phlebotomy modules): Lab orders and results, microbiology cultures and sensitivities, pathology reports, blood bank/transfusion records, radiology orders and reports, imaging documentation, phlebotomy collection records.\n\n**Pharmacy data** (per Pharmacy module): Medication dispensing records, pharmacy orders, formulary data, e-prescribing transactions (Surescripts integration implied by CPOE/e-prescribing certification criteria).\n\n**Revenue cycle and financial data** (per Revenue Cycle, Practice Management, Registration, Abstracting, Electronic Claims, Scheduling): Patient registration and insurance information, scheduling data, referral tracking, charge capture, claims/billing data, electronic claims submissions, patient accounts, collections, coding/abstracting data, HIM records.\n\n**Patient engagement data** (per MyHealth portal, Patient Connect, Telehealth, Virtual Care): Patient portal messages, appointment requests, telehealth visit records, patient-generated health data, remote monitoring data, patient satisfaction/feedback data, two-way text messages.\n\n**Care coordination data** (per Care Compass, Case Management, Community Care Transitions, Traverse Exchange): Population health registries, care coordination records, case management notes, transition of care documents (C-CDAs), community care referral data, interoperability exchange records.\n\n**Business/administrative data** (per full Business Operations suite): General ledger entries, accounts payable, payroll/personnel records, human resources data, materials management/supply chain data, fixed assets, budgeting/forecasting data, staffing schedules.\n\n**Analytics/reporting data** (per BCA, Data Repository, Surveillance): Clinical and business analytics datasets, quality measures, risk management records, surveillance alerts, executive dashboards.\n\n**Document management** (per Scanning and Archiving, HIM): Scanned documents, archived records, release of information records.\n\nThe certification page also lists separately certified modules that interact with these platforms: Continuity of Care Interface (CCD) for clinical document exchange, MyHealth Portal for patient access, and multiple Public Health Interface modules (immunization registry, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, electronic case reporting, cancer case reporting). These imply structured data for immunizations, reportable conditions, syndromic surveillance, and cancer registry data.\n\n---\n\n## Product: MEDITECH 6.x (6.0 and 6.1)\n\nCHPL IDs: 10931 (6.1 Core HCIS), 10935 (6.1 ED Management), 10972 (6.0 Core HCIS), 10973 (6.0 ED Management), 11743 (6.1 Ambulatory)\n\n### What It Is\n\nMEDITECH 6.x is the prior-generation platform preceding Expanse. It represents two sub-versions: 6.0 (v6.08c) and 6.1 (v6.15c). Like Expanse, it is a comprehensive integrated hospital information system, not just a clinical EHR module. The 6.x platform was the bridge between MEDITECH's older Client/Server architecture and the modern web-based Expanse. It introduced improved graphical interfaces and some web capabilities but is not fully web-based like Expanse.\n\nThe certified modules (Core HCIS, ED Management, Ambulatory) are components of the full 6.x platform, which includes the same broad range of clinical, financial, and administrative modules described under Expanse (though with older user interfaces and fewer modern features like AI, mobile apps, and cloud hosting).\n\n### Users & Market\n\nMEDITECH 6.x is still in active use at many hospitals that have not yet migrated to Expanse. According to KLAS data, 63% of legacy MEDITECH customers making a new EHR decision in 2024 chose to migrate to Expanse, meaning a significant portion remain on 6.x or are in the process of transitioning. The user base includes community hospitals, regional health systems, and some larger organizations that adopted 6.x before Expanse was available. The same range of clinical and administrative staff use 6.x as use Expanse.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe 6.x platform includes functionally similar modules to Expanse — clinical EHR, lab, pharmacy, imaging, revenue cycle, practice management, business operations, etc. — but without Expanse's modern features (web-based UI, AI/ambient listening, mobile-first apps, cloud-native MaaS, advanced interoperability via Traverse Exchange). The certification page shows that 6.x deployments also use the same shared modules: Continuity of Care Interface (CCD), MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interfaces for immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, electronic case reporting, and cancer case reporting.\n\nThe 6.1 platform also has a separately certified Oncology module (MEDITECH 6.1 Oncology v6.15c), indicating integrated cancer care management.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nThe data stored by MEDITECH 6.x is essentially the same categories as Expanse (clinical records, lab results, pharmacy data, billing/claims, scheduling, patient portal messages, care coordination documents, business operations data, etc.), since it runs the same integrated suite of modules. The primary differences from Expanse are in the user interface, technology architecture, and modern feature set — not in the fundamental data domains.\n\n---\n\n## Product: MEDITECH MAGIC\n\nCHPL IDs: 10979 (MAGIC Core HCIS), 10981 (MAGIC ED Management), 11018 (MAGIC HCA Core HCIS without PatientKeeper)\n\n### What It Is\n\nMEDITECH MAGIC is the company's legacy platform, originally launched in 1979. It is built on the MUMPS programming language (also known as M/MIIS) and features a character-based \"green-screen\" user interface. Despite its age, MAGIC remains in active use at some hospitals, particularly those that have not yet migrated to newer MEDITECH platforms. MAGIC is an on-premises system and does not support web-based or mobile workflows.\n\nThe \"MAGIC HCA\" variant (CHPL 11018) is a special certification for HCA Healthcare's deployment of MAGIC without the PatientKeeper mobile overlay. This variant has notably different certified criteria from the standard MAGIC product, including (b)(1) Transitions of Care — Send, (b)(2) Transitions of Care — Receive, (e)(1) View/Download/Transmit (patient portal), and (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5) for public health reporting (immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, cancer case reporting). This suggests HCA's MAGIC deployment includes additional interoperability and patient-facing capabilities not present in the standard MAGIC certification.\n\nPatientKeeper is a third-party clinician-facing mobile application that integrates with MEDITECH systems (both MAGIC and Expanse) to provide physicians with smartphone/tablet access to patient data, lab results, notes, and workflow tools. HCA has historically used PatientKeeper as a mobile overlay on top of MAGIC's text-based interface.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nMAGIC's primary remaining user base consists of hospitals that have not yet migrated to newer MEDITECH platforms. The most notable MAGIC user is HCA Healthcare, which operated 190+ hospitals on MAGIC and is actively migrating to Expanse. As of 2024, HCA had completed Expanse rollout to 43+ hospitals. MAGIC is only receiving minimal updates for compliance and patient safety; it is no longer the focus of MEDITECH's development. The system is on a sunset trajectory.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nMAGIC, like all MEDITECH platforms, is a comprehensive integrated hospital information system. The certification page lists MAGIC-specific certifications for: Core HCIS, ED Management, Medical and Practice Management (MPM) — an ambulatory/practice management module, Oncology, and the shared CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules. MAGIC supports the full range of hospital operations — clinical documentation, lab, pharmacy, imaging, billing, scheduling, etc. — but through its legacy green-screen interface.\n\nThe MAGIC platform also includes the same Business Operations modules (GL, AP, HR, Payroll, Materials Management, etc.) as the broader MEDITECH suite, as these are part of the integrated HCIS platform.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nMAGIC stores the same fundamental categories of healthcare data as the newer platforms: clinical records, lab results, pharmacy data, orders, billing/claims, scheduling, patient registration, supply chain, HR/payroll, etc. The data model is rooted in MUMPS/M databases rather than modern relational or cloud databases, but the functional data domains are comparable. The HCA variant's additional certifications for (e)(1) patient portal access and (b)(1)/(b)(2) transitions of care confirm that patient portal data and clinical document exchange data are also stored.\n\n---\n\n## Product: MEDITECH Client/Server\n\nCHPL IDs: 10982 (C/S Core HCIS), 10984 (C/S ED Management)\n\n### What It Is\n\nMEDITECH Client/Server (C/S) is the platform generation that succeeded MAGIC in the 1990s. It introduced a graphical thick-client architecture replacing MAGIC's green-screen interface, but it is still a legacy platform that predates the web-based 6.x and Expanse systems. Like MAGIC, Client/Server is on-premises only and is receiving only minimal compliance updates. It shares the v5.67c software version with MAGIC, suggesting convergence in the codebase at this point.\n\nThe certification page lists Client/Server certifications for: Core HCIS, ED Management, Medical and Practice Management (MPM), Oncology, and the shared CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nClient/Server's user base consists of hospitals that adopted MEDITECH after MAGIC but before 6.x. Like MAGIC users, many are migrating to Expanse. The system is on a sunset trajectory with minimal development investment.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nClient/Server includes the same comprehensive suite of integrated hospital modules as other MEDITECH platforms — clinical EHR, lab, pharmacy, imaging, revenue cycle, practice management, business operations, etc. — through a graphical thick-client interface. The certification page confirms C/S also has separately certified Oncology, MPM, CCD, MyHealth Portal, and Public Health Interface modules.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nData domains are comparable to the other MEDITECH platforms: clinical records, diagnostic results, pharmacy data, billing/revenue cycle, scheduling, administrative/business data. The data architecture is legacy but the functional scope is the same integrated HCIS.\n\n---\n\n## Cross-Platform Observations\n\n### Shared Components\nAll four platforms share certain separately certified modules that are cross-compatible:\n- **MEDITECH Continuity of Care Interface (CCD)** — handles clinical document exchange (C-CDAs)\n- **MEDITECH MyHealth Portal** (v2.0c) — patient portal for all platforms\n- **Public Health Interfaces** — immunization registry transmission, syndromic surveillance, reportable lab results, electronic case reporting, cancer case reporting\n\n### Certification Criteria Analysis\nAll products have nearly identical certified criteria, including:\n- **(a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)**: Core clinical capabilities (CPOE, demographics, problem list, medication list, medication allergy list, family health history, implantable device list)\n- **(b)(3), (b)(10), (b)(11)**: Electronic prescribing, EHI export, care plan interoperability\n- **(c)(1)–(c)(3)**: Clinical quality measures\n- **(d) series**: Privacy, security, auditing\n- **(e)(3)**: Patient health information capture\n- **(g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)**: API access, FHIR\n\nThe MAGIC HCA variant (11018) uniquely includes (b)(1), (b)(2) transitions of care, (e)(1) patient portal access, and (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5) public health reporting — suggesting HCA's deployment has enhanced interoperability capabilities.\n\n### Key Takeaway for EHI Export Assessment\nThe certified modules (Core HCIS, Ambulatory, ED Management) are just three components of an enormous integrated hospital information system. The full MEDITECH platform includes dozens of modules spanning clinical care, diagnostics, pharmacy, revenue cycle, patient engagement, care coordination, specialty care (oncology, L&D, mental health, home health, hospice, surgical services, critical care, dietary), and business operations (GL, AP, HR, payroll, materials management, staffing). Under §170.315(b)(10), the EHI export should cover all electronic health information stored by the product \"of which the Health IT Module is a part\" — meaning the entire integrated MEDITECH system, not just the certified module.\n","numLines":247,"startLine":1,"totalLines":247}}}

The documentation describes a zip-file-based export mechanism explicitly designed for §170.315(b)(10) compliance — this is clearly a purposeful (b)(10) implementation, not a repackaged FHIR API.\n    76→\n    77→### Export Architecture\n    78→\n    79→MEDITECH uses **two export configurations** depending on the platform and which internal modules are deployed:\n    80→\n    81→**Configuration 1** (Expanse 2.2, Expanse 2.1, 6.15, 6.08 Acute, Client/Server Acute, MAGIC Acute):\n    82→- Uses Health Information Management (HIM) and Scanning & Archiving with eChart (SCN)\n    83→- Produces a zip containing:\n    84→  - **Electronic Chart documents** — scanned/electronic documents organized by account, category, and subcategory (images: PNG, JPG, TIF, BMP; documents: PDF)\n    85→  - **US Core FHIR Resources.json** — Patient $everything bundle (FHIR R4, US Core STU 3.1.1)\n    86→  - **C-CDA documents** — Consolidated-CDA documents (R2.1 or R1.1)\n    87→  - **Financial reports** — Patient accounting transactions (FinancialEHI.txt), resident trust (Expanse/6.1x), cost estimation (Expanse only)\n    88→  - **Supplemental sections** varying by version: ambulatory results, authorization/referral management, immunization history, population health, utilization review, provider messages, implantable devices, patient notices, historical ambulatory data, ambulatory order summary\n    89→\n    90→**Configuration 2** (MPM 6.08 Ambulatory, Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory, MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory):\n    91→- Uses Medical Records (MRI) and Data Repository (DR)\n    92→- Produces a zip containing:\n    93→  - **Patient Data CSV files** — structured tabular data with named columns, one file per namespace\n    94→  - **FHIR Resources.json** — Patient $everything bundle\n    95→  - **C-CDA documents**\n    96→  - **Clinical Reports/Documents** — physician documentation, radiology, pathology, nursing images\n    97→  - **Provider and Patient Messages**\n    98→  - **Financial Reports** (FinancialEHI.txt)\n    99→  - **External Documents/Images** — scanned/imported documents\n   100→  - **Point of Contact Scanned Documents**\n   101→\n   102→### Export Metadata\n   103→\n   104→Both configurations include machine-readable metadata:\n   105→- **README.txt** — Table of contents with folder/file descriptions\n   106→- **EHIEXPORTSCHEMA.txt** (Config 1) / **SCHEMA.txt** (Config 2) — Documentation URL and product version\n   107→- **Table of Contents.ndjson** (Config 1) / **JSONTOC.txt** (Config 2) — NDJSON file containing FHIR DocumentReference resources conforming to the Argonaut EHI Export API IG (draft). 141→## Export Coverage Assessment\n   142→\n   143→### Data Domain Coverage\n   144→\n   145→MEDITECH has taken the (b)(10) requirement seriously. The export is clearly distinct from their (g)(10) FHIR API — while it includes a US Core FHIR bundle, it goes well beyond that with supplemental data exports in multiple formats.\n   146→\n   147→**Domains clearly covered:**\n   148→- **Demographics & patient data**: Extensive coverage via Adm tables (admissions, visits, insurance, employers, next of kin, guarantors) and FHIR Patient resource\n   149→- **Clinical notes & documentation**: Electronic chart documents (Config 1), physician/nursing documentation (Config 2), radiology/pathology reports\n   150→- **Diagnoses & problems**: AdmVisitDiagnoses, problem lists in FHIR bundle and ambulatory data\n   151→- **Medications & prescriptions**: Rxm (prescription) tables, Pha (pharmacy) tables, FHIR MedicationRequest\n   152→- **Lab results**: Lab tables, FHIR Observation/DiagnosticReport, ambulatory results PDFs\n   153→- **Imaging/Radiology**: Rad tables (MAGIC), radiology reports in clinical documents\n   154→- **Pathology**: Pth tables, pathology report documents\n   155→- **Blood bank**: Bbk tables (C/S, MAGIC)\n   156→- **Microbiology**: Mic tables\n   157→- **Immunizations**: Dedicated section with immunization history\n   158→- **Allergies**: Via FHIR AllergyIntolerance resource\n   159→- **Vital signs**: Sch vital signs tables, FHIR Observation\n   160→- **Financial/billing data**: FinancialEHI.txt with patient accounting transactions, ResidentTrustEHI.txt, CostEstimation.txt, insurance data\n   161→- **Authorization & referral management**: ARM reports (Expanse/6.x), Arm tables (legacy)\n   162→- **Provider/patient messages**: Provider Messages section (C/S, MAGIC), Patient Notices (6.08)\n   163→- **C-CDA documents**: All structured clinical documents created by the system\n   164→- **Implantable devices**: Dedicated section (C/S, MAGIC)\n   165→- **Population health**: External aggregated data (Expanse/6.x)\n   166→- **Utilization review**: Case management utilization reviews (Expanse/6.x)\n   167→- **Care coordination**: Care Compass data via population health section\n   168→- **Scanned/external documents**: AmbScans, Point of Contact scans, Electronic Chart document images\n   169→- **ED-specific data**: Edm tables, encounter data\n   170→- **Nursing documentation**: Nur tables (C/S, MAGIC)\n   171→- **Order entry**: Oe tables\n   172→- **Ambulatory-specific data**: AprEnc encounter data, AprPat patient data, ambulatory results, historical ambulatory data\n   173→\n   174→**Domains with potential gaps or ambiguity:**\n   175→- **Specialty clinical data** (oncology, mental health, L&D, surgical services, critical care, dietary): The product research shows MEDITECH stores extensive specialty-specific data across many modules (Oncology, Mental Health, Labor & Delivery, Surgical Services, Critical Care, Dietary). The export documentation does not specifically mention these specialty modules. The Electronic Chart documents and FHIR bundle might capture some of this, but there is no explicit mention of specialty-specific data exports (e.g., oncology treatment protocols, mental health assessments, L&D records).\n   176→- **Home health and hospice**: MEDITECH offers Home Health and Hospice modules, but the export documentation does not reference them.\n   177→- **Telehealth visit records**: Not mentioned in the export documentation.\n   178→- **Patient portal content** (beyond provider messages): MyHealth portal data beyond provider messages is not explicitly documented.\n   179→- **Care plan data**: The FHIR bundle may include CarePlan resources via US Core, but there is no explicit mention of detailed care plan exports.\n   180→\n   181→**Not expected in export (correctly scoped):**\n   182→- System configuration, audit logs, quality metrics, provider credentialing, staff scheduling, template definitions — these are correctly excluded as non-EHI.\n   183→\n   184→### Export Format & Standards\n   185→\n   186→MEDITECH uses a multi-format approach that is well-suited to the breadth of data:\n   187→\n   188→- **CSV files** (Config 2): Tabular data from the Data Repository. The format is well-documented with three PDF data dictionaries listing every table name, field label, and database column. However, the documentation does **not** include data types, value sets, cardinality, or relationships between tables.\n   189→- **FHIR R4 JSON** (both configs): US Core STU 3.1.1 Patient $everything bundle. Standard clinical document format.\n   191→- **PDF/TXT/image files** (both configs): Clinical reports, financial reports, immunization records, pathology reports, and scanned documents in various formats.\n   192→- **NDJSON metadata** (both configs): FHIR DocumentReference resources indexing every file in the zip, conforming to the Argonaut EHI Export API IG draft.\n   193→\n   194→The multi-format approach is reasonable — MEDITECH is exporting each data type in the format most natural to it rather than forcing everything into a single standard. CSV for structured tabular data, FHIR for US Core clinical data, C-CDA for clinical documents, and native formats for reports/images.\n   195→\n   196→A third party could reconstruct a substantial portion of the patient record from this export, though the CSV data lacks relationship documentation — a consumer would need to infer foreign key relationships from naming conventions (e.g., column names ending in \"ID\").\n   197→\n   198→### Documentation Quality\n   199→\n   200→**Strengths:**\n   201→- The HTML documentation is well-organized with separate pages for each configuration\n   202→- The platform-specific section matrix clearly shows what each product version exports\n   203→- The export zip structure is documented down to folder names and file naming conventions\n   204→- The NDJSON table-of-contents schema is documented with field-level descriptions\n   205→- The CSV data dictionaries are comprehensive — 752 tables, 2,834 fields across three platform PDFs\n   206→\n   207→**Weaknesses:**\n   208→- CSV data dictionaries list only Field (human label), Table name, and Column name. There are **no data types, value sets, cardinality constraints, or field descriptions**. A consumer knows that `AdmVisits.RaceID` exists but not what values it can take or what its type is.\n   209→- **No sample data or example export files** are provided. A developer has no concrete example of what the zip file looks like or what the CSV content looks like.\n   210→- Relationships between tables are not documented. The CSV tables clearly have foreign-key relationships (columns ending in \"ID\"), but these are not described.\n   211→- The Configuration 1 documentation describes sections at a high level (e.g., \"Electronic Chart contains documents organized by category/subcategory\") but has **no data dictionary** — it's entirely a folder/file format description. The actual clinical data content for Config 1 is largely described only as \"US Core FHIR resources\" and \"C-CDA documents.\"\n   212→- The \"Regulatory EHI Export Functionality Guides\" that would provide implementation guidance are behind a customer login wall.\n   213→\n   214→### Structure & Completeness\n   215→\n   216→- **Config 2 CSV data dictionaries**: Granular at the field level (table + column), but lack data types, descriptions, value sets, and relationships. This is a data dictionary in name but effectively just a column listing.\n   217→- **Config 1**: No comparable data dictionary at all. The export content beyond FHIR/C-CDA is described only in terms of folder structure and file types.\n   218→- **FHIR bundle**: Documented by reference to US Core STU 3.1.1 and fhir.meditech.com — standard and well-understood.\n   219→- **C-CDA documents**: Documented by reference to C-CDA R2.1/R1.1 — standard.\n   220→- **No versioning or change history** on the documentation itself (PDFs note \"Last Updated: October 2023\").\n   221→\n   222→### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n   223→\n   224→MEDITECH's approach clearly demonstrates awareness of the distinction between (b)(10) and (g)(10):\n   225→\n   226→1. The export is explicitly labeled as §170.315(b)(10) and described as producing a zip file (not an API endpoint)\n   227→2. The export includes financial data, provider messages, scanned documents, pathology, blood bank, nursing documentation, and other data that goes well beyond US Core/USCDI\n   228→3. The FHIR bundle is included as one component alongside other exports, not as the sole mechanism\n   229→4. The CSV data dictionaries for legacy platforms document hundreds of database tables covering clinical, administrative, and financial domains\n   230→\n   231→This is **not** a repackaged (g)(10) FHIR API. MEDITECH has built a genuine (b)(10) bulk export that attempts to cover the designated record set.\n   232→\n   233→The primary concern is **completeness for Configuration 1 platforms** (Expanse, 6.x). For these newer platforms, the export relies heavily on the Electronic Chart (scanned/electronic documents) plus FHIR plus C-CDA. There are no CSV data dictionaries or structured tabular exports for Config 1. This may mean that data stored in structured database tables — beyond what US Core covers — is only exported as rendered documents (PDFs, images) rather than as structured data. A scanned lab result image is less useful than a structured lab result table.\n   234→\n   235→For Configuration 2 (legacy platforms), the CSV export with 300+ tables provides genuinely comprehensive structured data coverage.\n   236→\n   237→## Access Summary\n   238→- Final URL (after redirects): https://home.meditech.com/en/d/restapiresources/pages/ehiexport.htm (no redirects)\n   239→- Status: found\n   240→- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n   241→- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page links to two config pages, config 2 links to PDFs)\n   242→- Anti-bot issues: none\n   243→\n   244→## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n   245→\n   246→- **Customer portal**: The \"Regulatory EHI Export Functionality Guides\" at `customer.meditech.com/en/d/21stcenturycuresact/pages/ehiexport.htm` requires SAML authentication. This documentation is not publicly accessible, which may violate the public accessibility expectation for (b)(10) documentation. (MEDITECH) — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-17\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://home.meditech.com/en/d/restapiresources/pages/ehiexport.htm\n- CHPL IDs: 10925, 10926, 10927, 10929, 10930, 10931, 10935, 10972, 10973, 10979, 10981, 10982, 10984, 11018, 11742, 11743\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n### Step 1: Initial probe of registered URL\n\n```bash\ncurl -sI -L \"https://home.meditech.com/en/d/restapiresources/pages/ehiexport.htm\" \\\n  -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (X11; Linux x86_64) AppleWebKit/537.36'\n```\n\nResult: HTTP 200, Content-Type: text/html, 8964 bytes. The documentation is not publicly accessible.\n\n```bash\ncurl -sI -L \"https://customer.meditech.com/en/d/21stcenturycuresact/pages/ehiexport.htm\"\n# Returns HTTP 302 -> Keycloak SAML login page\n```\n\n### Step 5: Download CSV data dictionary PDFs from Configuration 2\n\nConfiguration 2 links to three PDF data dictionaries:\n\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://home.meditech.com/en/d/regulatoryresources/otherfiles/608ehiexportcsv.pdf\" -o 608ehiexportcsv.pdf\ncurl -sL \"https://home.meditech.com/en/d/regulatoryresources/otherfiles/csacuteandambehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\" -o csacuteandambehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\ncurl -sL \"https://home.meditech.com/en/d/regulatoryresources/otherfiles/mgehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\" -o mgehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\n```\n\nAll three downloaded as valid PDF documents:\n- `608ehiexportcsv.pdf`: 19 pages, 251 KB — MPM 6.08 Ambulatory CSV data dictionary\n- `csacuteandambehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf`: 20 pages, 263 KB — Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory CSV data dictionary\n- `mgehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf`: 20 pages, 364 KB — MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory CSV data dictionary\n\n### Step 6: Fetch the \"old\" EHI Export page\n\nConfig pages reference `/en/d/restapiresources/pages/ehiexportold.htm` as the original EHI Export homepage. Fetched and saved; it contains essentially the same information as the current Config 1 page (same section structure, same descriptions).\n\n### Step 7: Take screenshots\n\nNavigated to each of the three main pages in Chrome and captured full-page screenshots.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nMEDITECH's EHI export documentation is well-structured, covering **four generations of their platform** across **16 certified products** under a single registered URL. The documentation describes a zip-file-based export mechanism explicitly designed for §170.315(b)(10) compliance — this is clearly a purposeful (b)(10) implementation, not a repackaged FHIR API.\n\n### Export Architecture\n\nMEDITECH uses **two export configurations** depending on the platform and which internal modules are deployed:\n\n**Configuration 1** (Expanse 2.2, Expanse 2.1, 6.15, 6.08 Acute, Client/Server Acute, MAGIC Acute):\n- Uses Health Information Management (HIM) and Scanning & Archiving with eChart (SCN)\n- Produces a zip containing:\n  - **Electronic Chart documents** — scanned/electronic documents organized by account, category, and subcategory (images: PNG, JPG, TIF, BMP; documents: PDF)\n  - **US Core FHIR Resources.json** — Patient $everything bundle (FHIR R4, US Core STU 3.1.1)\n  - **C-CDA documents** — Consolidated-CDA documents (R2.1 or R1.1)\n  - **Financial reports** — Patient accounting transactions (FinancialEHI.txt), resident trust (Expanse/6.1x), cost estimation (Expanse only)\n  - **Supplemental sections** varying by version: ambulatory results, authorization/referral management, immunization history, population health, utilization review, provider messages, implantable devices, patient notices, historical ambulatory data, ambulatory order summary\n\n**Configuration 2** (MPM 6.08 Ambulatory, Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory, MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory):\n- Uses Medical Records (MRI) and Data Repository (DR)\n- Produces a zip containing:\n  - **Patient Data CSV files** — structured tabular data with named columns, one file per namespace\n  - **FHIR Resources.json** — Patient $everything bundle\n  - **C-CDA documents**\n  - **Clinical Reports/Documents** — physician documentation, radiology, pathology, nursing images\n  - **Provider and Patient Messages**\n  - **Financial Reports** (FinancialEHI.txt)\n  - **External Documents/Images** — scanned/imported documents\n  - **Point of Contact Scanned Documents**\n\n### Export Metadata\n\nBoth configurations include machine-readable metadata:\n- **README.txt** — Table of contents with folder/file descriptions\n- **EHIEXPORTSCHEMA.txt** (Config 1) / **SCHEMA.txt** (Config 2) — Documentation URL and product version\n- **Table of Contents.ndjson** (Config 1) / **JSONTOC.txt** (Config 2) — NDJSON file containing FHIR DocumentReference resources conforming to the Argonaut EHI Export API IG (draft). Each entry describes a file in the zip with its content type, path, size, creation date, encounter reference, and time period.\n- **ACCOUNTS_INDEX.html/xml** (Config 1) — Browser-viewable index for human readability\n\n### CSV Data Dictionaries (Configuration 2 only)\n\nThree PDF data dictionaries document the tables/columns exported as CSV:\n\n| Platform | Tables | Fields |\n|----------|--------|--------|\n| MPM 6.08 Ambulatory | 97 | 482 |\n| Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory | 333 | 1,231 |\n| MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory | 322 | 1,121 |\n\nKey table name prefixes and the domains they represent:\n- **Adm** (Admissions): demographics, insurance, next of kin, diagnoses, employers, guarantors, clinical queries, discharge info\n- **Sch** (Scheduling/Care): vital signs, appointments, assessments, interventions\n- **Mri** (Medical Records): chart documents, medical records data\n- **Nur** (Nursing): nursing documentation, interventions, assessments\n- **Pha** (Pharmacy): pharmacy orders, dispensing, medication records\n- **Apr** (Ambulatory/Practice): encounters, messages, vitals, problem lists, tasks, health maintenance\n- **Rxm** (Prescriptions): prescriptions, medication orders, queries\n- **Arm** (Authorization/Referral Management): authorizations, referrals, insurance eligibility\n- **Its** (Interface Transaction Services): likely interoperability/exchange data\n- **Bbk** (Blood Bank): blood bank/transfusion data\n- **Pth** (Pathology): pathology reports, specimens\n- **Edm** (ED Management): emergency department data\n- **Lab** (Laboratory): lab results, tests\n- **Mic** (Microbiology): microbiology cultures, sensitivities\n- **Pbr** (Patient Billing/Records): billing records, financial data\n- **Oe** (Order Entry): orders\n- **Rad** (Radiology): radiology exams, reports (MAGIC)\n- **Eps** (E-Prescribing): electronic prescribing (MAGIC)\n- **Hub** (Hub): interoperability hub data (C/S)\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nMEDITECH has taken the (b)(10) requirement seriously. The export is clearly distinct from their (g)(10) FHIR API — while it includes a US Core FHIR bundle, it goes well beyond that with supplemental data exports in multiple formats.\n\n**Domains clearly covered:**\n- **Demographics & patient data**: Extensive coverage via Adm tables (admissions, visits, insurance, employers, next of kin, guarantors) and FHIR Patient resource\n- **Clinical notes & documentation**: Electronic chart documents (Config 1), physician/nursing documentation (Config 2), radiology/pathology reports\n- **Diagnoses & problems**: AdmVisitDiagnoses, problem lists in FHIR bundle and ambulatory data\n- **Medications & prescriptions**: Rxm (prescription) tables, Pha (pharmacy) tables, FHIR MedicationRequest\n- **Lab results**: Lab tables, FHIR Observation/DiagnosticReport, ambulatory results PDFs\n- **Imaging/Radiology**: Rad tables (MAGIC), radiology reports in clinical documents\n- **Pathology**: Pth tables, pathology report documents\n- **Blood bank**: Bbk tables (C/S, MAGIC)\n- **Microbiology**: Mic tables\n- **Immunizations**: Dedicated section with immunization history\n- **Allergies**: Via FHIR AllergyIntolerance resource\n- **Vital signs**: Sch vital signs tables, FHIR Observation\n- **Financial/billing data**: FinancialEHI.txt with patient accounting transactions, ResidentTrustEHI.txt, CostEstimation.txt, insurance data\n- **Authorization & referral management**: ARM reports (Expanse/6.x), Arm tables (legacy)\n- **Provider/patient messages**: Provider Messages section (C/S, MAGIC), Patient Notices (6.08)\n- **C-CDA documents**: All structured clinical documents created by the system\n- **Implantable devices**: Dedicated section (C/S, MAGIC)\n- **Population health**: External aggregated data (Expanse/6.x)\n- **Utilization review**: Case management utilization reviews (Expanse/6.x)\n- **Care coordination**: Care Compass data via population health section\n- **Scanned/external documents**: AmbScans, Point of Contact scans, Electronic Chart document images\n- **ED-specific data**: Edm tables, encounter data\n- **Nursing documentation**: Nur tables (C/S, MAGIC)\n- **Order entry**: Oe tables\n- **Ambulatory-specific data**: AprEnc encounter data, AprPat patient data, ambulatory results, historical ambulatory data\n\n**Domains with potential gaps or ambiguity:**\n- **Specialty clinical data** (oncology, mental health, L&D, surgical services, critical care, dietary): The product research shows MEDITECH stores extensive specialty-specific data across many modules (Oncology, Mental Health, Labor & Delivery, Surgical Services, Critical Care, Dietary). The export documentation does not specifically mention these specialty modules. The Electronic Chart documents and FHIR bundle might capture some of this, but there is no explicit mention of specialty-specific data exports (e.g., oncology treatment protocols, mental health assessments, L&D records).\n- **Home health and hospice**: MEDITECH offers Home Health and Hospice modules, but the export documentation does not reference them.\n- **Telehealth visit records**: Not mentioned in the export documentation.\n- **Patient portal content** (beyond provider messages): MyHealth portal data beyond provider messages is not explicitly documented.\n- **Care plan data**: The FHIR bundle may include CarePlan resources via US Core, but there is no explicit mention of detailed care plan exports.\n\n**Not expected in export (correctly scoped):**\n- System configuration, audit logs, quality metrics, provider credentialing, staff scheduling, template definitions — these are correctly excluded as non-EHI.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nMEDITECH uses a multi-format approach that is well-suited to the breadth of data:\n\n- **CSV files** (Config 2): Tabular data from the Data Repository. The format is well-documented with three PDF data dictionaries listing every table name, field label, and database column. However, the documentation does **not** include data types, value sets, cardinality, or relationships between tables.\n- **FHIR R4 JSON** (both configs): US Core STU 3.1.1 Patient $everything bundle. This covers standard clinical data (conditions, medications, allergies, observations, etc.). Documentation points to fhir.meditech.com for resource-level details.\n- **C-CDA XML** (both configs): Consolidated-CDA documents (R2.1 or R1.1). Standard clinical document format.\n- **PDF/TXT/image files** (both configs): Clinical reports, financial reports, immunization records, pathology reports, and scanned documents in various formats.\n- **NDJSON metadata** (both configs): FHIR DocumentReference resources indexing every file in the zip, conforming to the Argonaut EHI Export API IG draft.\n\nThe multi-format approach is reasonable — MEDITECH is exporting each data type in the format most natural to it rather than forcing everything into a single standard. CSV for structured tabular data, FHIR for US Core clinical data, C-CDA for clinical documents, and native formats for reports/images.\n\nA third party could reconstruct a substantial portion of the patient record from this export, though the CSV data lacks relationship documentation — a consumer would need to infer foreign key relationships from naming conventions (e.g., column names ending in \"ID\").\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The HTML documentation is well-organized with separate pages for each configuration\n- The platform-specific section matrix clearly shows what each product version exports\n- The export zip structure is documented down to folder names and file naming conventions\n- The NDJSON table-of-contents schema is documented with field-level descriptions\n- The CSV data dictionaries are comprehensive — 752 tables, 2,834 fields across three platform PDFs\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- CSV data dictionaries list only Field (human label), Table name, and Column name. There are **no data types, value sets, cardinality constraints, or field descriptions**. A consumer knows that `AdmVisits.RaceID` exists but not what values it can take or what its type is.\n- **No sample data or example export files** are provided. A developer has no concrete example of what the zip file looks like or what the CSV content looks like.\n- Relationships between tables are not documented. The CSV tables clearly have foreign-key relationships (columns ending in \"ID\"), but these are not described.\n- The Configuration 1 documentation describes sections at a high level (e.g., \"Electronic Chart contains documents organized by category/subcategory\") but has **no data dictionary** — it's entirely a folder/file format description. The actual clinical data content for Config 1 is largely described only as \"US Core FHIR resources\" and \"C-CDA documents.\"\n- The \"Regulatory EHI Export Functionality Guides\" that would provide implementation guidance are behind a customer login wall.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Config 2 CSV data dictionaries**: Granular at the field level (table + column), but lack data types, descriptions, value sets, and relationships. This is a data dictionary in name but effectively just a column listing.\n- **Config 1**: No comparable data dictionary at all. The export content beyond FHIR/C-CDA is described only in terms of folder structure and file types.\n- **FHIR bundle**: Documented by reference to US Core STU 3.1.1 and fhir.meditech.com — standard and well-understood.\n- **C-CDA documents**: Documented by reference to C-CDA R2.1/R1.1 — standard.\n- **No versioning or change history** on the documentation itself (PDFs note \"Last Updated: October 2023\").\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nMEDITECH's approach clearly demonstrates awareness of the distinction between (b)(10) and (g)(10):\n\n1. The export is explicitly labeled as §170.315(b)(10) and described as producing a zip file (not an API endpoint)\n2. The export includes financial data, provider messages, scanned documents, pathology, blood bank, nursing documentation, and other data that goes well beyond US Core/USCDI\n3. The FHIR bundle is included as one component alongside other exports, not as the sole mechanism\n4. The CSV data dictionaries for legacy platforms document hundreds of database tables covering clinical, administrative, and financial domains\n\nThis is **not** a repackaged (g)(10) FHIR API. MEDITECH has built a genuine (b)(10) bulk export that attempts to cover the designated record set.\n\nThe primary concern is **completeness for Configuration 1 platforms** (Expanse, 6.x). For these newer platforms, the export relies heavily on the Electronic Chart (scanned/electronic documents) plus FHIR plus C-CDA. There are no CSV data dictionaries or structured tabular exports for Config 1. This may mean that data stored in structured database tables — beyond what US Core covers — is only exported as rendered documents (PDFs, images) rather than as structured data. A scanned lab result image is less useful than a structured lab result table.\n\nFor Configuration 2 (legacy platforms), the CSV export with 300+ tables provides genuinely comprehensive structured data coverage.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://home.meditech.com/en/d/restapiresources/pages/ehiexport.htm (no redirects)\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page links to two config pages, config 2 links to PDFs)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **Customer portal**: The \"Regulatory EHI Export Functionality Guides\" at `customer.meditech.com/en/d/21stcenturycuresact/pages/ehiexport.htm` requires SAML authentication. This documentation is not publicly accessible, which may violate the public accessibility expectation for (b)(10) documentation. The guides likely contain implementation instructions for healthcare organizations performing the export.\n- **No data dictionary for Config 1**: The newer platforms (Expanse, 6.x) under Configuration 1 have no equivalent of the CSV data dictionaries. The documentation for these platforms is limited to describing the folder/file structure and referencing FHIR/C-CDA standards.\n- **PDF text extraction**: The data dictionary PDFs use layout-based table rendering. Text extraction with `pdftotext -layout` works well, but the column structure requires careful parsing.\n","numLines":249,"startLine":1,"totalLines":249}}}

456→</ul>\n   457→</td>\n   458→</tr>\n   459→</tbody>\n   460→</table>\n   461→<p><br /></p>\n   462→<h3><a name=\"JSONTOC.ndjson\" id=\"JSONTOC.ndjson\" class=\"mceItemAnchor wuanchor\" data-wu-link=\"anchor\"></a>JSONTOC Schema</h3>\n   463→<p>The ndjson file contains an array of file metadata (FHIR Document Reference resources) for all of the patient data files contained within the export zip file.&nbsp; The pseudo-JSON below explains what each field represents.</p>\n   464→<p><code>{ &nbsp; &nbsp;<br />&nbsp; &nbsp;\"resourceType\":\"DocumentReference\",<br />&nbsp; &nbsp;\"id\": &lt;&lt;Identifier of this DocumentReference&gt;&gt;,<br />&nbsp; &nbsp;\"meta\": {<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"id\":&nbsp;&lt;&lt;Identifier of this metadata&gt;&gt;,<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"versionId\": &lt;&lt;Identifier of this version&gt;&gt;,<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"lastUpdated\": &lt;&lt;When this was last updated&gt;&gt;,<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"source\": &lt;&lt;Where the resource came from&gt;&gt;,<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"profile\": [<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp;\"http://fhir.org/argonaut/ehi-api/StructureDefinition/ehi-document-reference\"<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; ],<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; \"tag\": [<br />&nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp;{<br 608ehiexportcsv.pdf (MPM 6.08 Ambulatory)\n *   - csacuteandambehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf (Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory)\n *   - mgehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf (MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory)\n *\n * Output: csv-data-dictionaries.json\n */\n\nimport { execSync } from \"child_process\";\nimport { resolve, dirname } from \"path\";\nimport { writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\n\nconst scriptDir = dirname(new URL(import.meta.url).pathname);\nconst downloadsDir = resolve(scriptDir, \"..\");\n\ninterface FieldEntry {\n  field: string;\n  table: string;\n  column: string;\n}\n\ninterface TableGroup {\n  tableName: string;\n  fields: FieldEntry[];\n}\n\ninterface PdfResult {\n  sourceFile: string;\n  platform: string;\n  lastUpdated: string;\n  tables: TableGroup[];\n  totalFields: number;\n  totalTables: number;\n  parseErrors: string[];\n}\n\nfunction extractTextFromPdf(pdfPath: string): string {\n  return execSync(`pdftotext -layout \"${pdfPath}\" -`, {\n    encoding: \"utf-8\",\n    maxBuffer: 10 * 1024 * 1024,\n  });\n}\n\nfunction parseCsvDataDictionary(text: string, sourceFile: string): PdfResult {\n  const lines = text.split(\"\\n\");\n  const parseErrors: string[] = [];\n\n  // Extract metadata from header\n  let platform = \"\";\n  let lastUpdated = \"\";\n  for (const line of lines.slice(0, 15)) {\n    const trimmed = line.trim();\n    if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Platform:\")) {\n      platform = trimmed.replace(\"Platform:\", \"\").trim();\n    }\n    if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Last Updated:\")) {\n      lastUpdated = trimmed.replace(\"Last Updated:\", \"\").trim();\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Find the header row \"Field ... Table ... Column\"\n  let dataStartIdx = -1;\n  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {\n    const trimmed = lines[i].trim();\n    if (/^Field\\s+Table\\s+Column\\s*$/.test(trimmed)) {\n      dataStartIdx = i + 1;\n      break;\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (dataStartIdx === -1) {\n    parseErrors.push(\"Could not find 'Field Table Column' header row\");\n    return {\n      sourceFile,\n      platform,\n      lastUpdated,\n      tables: [],\n      totalFields: 0,\n      totalTables: 0,\n      parseErrors,\n    };\n  }\n\n  const tables: TableGroup[] = [];\n  let currentTable: TableGroup | null = null;\n\n  for (let i = dataStartIdx; i < lines.length; i++) {\n    const line = lines[i];\n    const trimmed = line.trim();\n\n    // Skip blank lines, page numbers, and repeated headers\n    if (!trimmed) continue;\n    if (/^\\d+$/.test(trimmed)) continue; // page number\n    if (/^MEDITECH$/i.test(trimmed)) continue;\n    if (/EHI Export Data in CSV File/i.test(trimmed)) continue;\n    if (/Last Updated:/i.test(trimmed)) continue;\n    if (/^Field\\s+Table\\s+Column\\s*$/.test(trimmed)) continue; // repeated header\n\n    // Detect table header lines: a single word/phrase with no whitespace-separated columns\n    // Table headers appear alone on a line, left-aligned, with no other columns\n    // A data row has at least two whitespace-separated segments\n    const segments = trimmed.split(/\\s{2,}/);\n\n    if (segments.length === 1) {\n      // This is a table header (or a continuation line — we'll treat single-segment as header)\n      // But check: could it be a data row where field, table, column happen to be the same word?\n      // In practice, table headers are PascalCase identifiers like \"AdmEmployers\"\n      const name = segments[0];\n      currentTable = { tableName: name, fields: [] };\n      tables.push(currentTable);\n    } else if (segments.length === 2) {\n      // Two segments: could be table + column (field missing), or field + table (column missing)\n      // Looking at the data, this pattern is: Table, Column (field name is the same as table header)\n      if (currentTable) {\n        currentTable.fields.push({\n          field: segments[0],\n          table: currentTable.tableName,\n          column: segments[1],\n        });\n      } else {\n        parseErrors.push(`Line ${i + 1}: Two-segment line without active table: \"${trimmed}\"`);\n      }\n    } else if (segments.length >= 3) {\n      // Standard row: Field, Table, Column\n      const field = segments[0];\n      const table = segments[1];\n      const column = segments.slice(2).join(\" \"); // column might have spaces\n      if (currentTable && table !== currentTable.tableName) {\n        // Table changed mid-section without a header — create new table\n        currentTable = { tableName: table, fields: [] };\n        tables.push(currentTable);\n      } else if (!currentTable) {\n        currentTable = { tableName: table, fields: [] };\n        tables.push(currentTable);\n      }\n      currentTable.fields.push({ field, table, column });\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Deduplicate tables by name (merge fields)\n  const tableMap = new Map<string, TableGroup>();\n  for (const t of tables) {\n    const existing = tableMap.get(t.tableName);\n    if (existing) {\n      existing.fields.push(...t.fields);\n    } else {\n      tableMap.set(t.tableName, { ...t });\n    }\n  }\n  const mergedTables = Array.from(tableMap.values());\n\n  const totalFields = mergedTables.reduce((sum, t) => sum + t.fields.length, 0);\n\n  return {\n    sourceFile,\n    platform,\n    lastUpdated,\n    tables: mergedTables,\n    totalFields,\n    totalTables: mergedTables.length,\n    parseErrors,\n  };\n}\n\n// Process all three PDFs\nconst pdfs = [\n  { file: \"608ehiexportcsv.pdf\", label: \"MPM 6.08 Ambulatory\" },\n  { file: \"csacuteandambehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\", label: \"Client/Server Acute & Ambulatory\" },\n  { file: \"mgehiexportdrsolutionmerged.pdf\", label: \"MAGIC Acute & Ambulatory\" },\n];\n\nconst results: PdfResult[] = [];\nconst stats = {\n  totalFilesDiscovered: pdfs.length,\n  totalFilesParsed: 0,\n  parseFailures: [] as { file: string; error: string }[],\n};\n\nfor (const pdf of pdfs) {\n  const pdfPath = resolve(downloadsDir, pdf.file);\n  try {\n    console.log(`Processing ${pdf.file}...`);\n    const text = extractTextFromPdf(pdfPath);\n    const result = parseCsvDataDictionary(text, pdf.file);\n    results.push(result);\n    stats.totalFilesParsed++;\n    console.log(`  Platform: ${result.platform}`);\n    console.log(`  Tables: ${result.totalTables}, Fields: ${result.totalFields}`);\n    if (result.parseErrors.length > 0) {\n      console.log(`  Parse errors: ${result.parseErrors.length}`);\n    }\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    stats.parseFailures.push({ file: pdf.file, error: e.message });\n    console.error(`  FAILED: ${e.message}`);\n  }\n}\n\nconst output = {\n  extractionDate: new Date().toISOString().split(\"T\")[0],\n  description: \"CSV data dictionaries extracted from MEDITECH EHI Export PDF files.
